Options: \"left\", \"right\", \"center\", \"baseline\".\n\nCurrently in the function:\n- `align in [\"left\", \"top\"]` → offsets = [d] (bottom of box at d)\n- `align in [\"right\", \"bottom\"]` → offsets = [height - h + d]\n\nFor VPacker, \"left\" → [d] is correct (left aligned), \"right\" → [height - w + d] is correct.\n\nFor HPacker, \"top\" → [d] aligns the bottoms (wrong — should align tops), \"bottom\" → [height - h + d] aligns the tops (wrong — should align bottoms).\n\nThe user's issue says top/bottom reversed for HPacker. So the fix: in HPacker, when align=\"top\", we want offsets = [height - h + d], and when align=\"bottom\", offsets = [d].\n\nBut the shared function maps \"left\"→[d] and \"right\"→[height-h+d] which is correct for VPacker. We can't just swap within the function without breaking VPacker.\n\nOptions:\n1. In HPacker, translate the align before passing: if align == \"top\" → pass something that yields [height-h+d]... but there's no such option except \"right\"/\"bottom\". Hmm, we could map align=\"top\" to \"bottom\" and align=\"bottom\" to \"top\" when calling from HPacker? That seems hacky and confusing.\n\nWait, let me reconsider. The docstring says \"For simplicity of the description, the terminology used here assumes a horizontal layout (i.e., vertical alignment)\".\n\nFor align=\"left\": offsets = [d]. In a horizontal layout with vertical alignment, \"left\" is the same as \"top\" (they're aliases!). So the function treats \"left\" and \"top\" as equivalent, and \"right\" and \"bottom\" as equivalent.\n\nNow, for VPacker (vertical packing, horizontal alignment), \"left\" → offsets=[d] gives left alignment ✓ and \"right\" → offsets=[width-w+d] gives right alignment ✓.\n\nFor HPacker (horizontal packing, vertical alignment), \"top\" → offsets=[d]. Hmm, but is this top or bottom alignment? \n\nThink of a horizontal layout: boxes side by side, aligned vertically. The \"height\" is the total height of the packing. A box with height h and descent d. The offset is the y-position of the bottom of the box relative to the bottom of the packing.\n\nalign=\"top\": all tops at the same level. Top of box = offset + h. For all boxes equal: offset = height - h. With descent: offset = height - h + d. So \"top\" should be offsets = [height - h + d] = the \"right\"/\"bottom\" branch.\n\nalign=\"bottom\": all bottoms at the same level (the bottom of the packing). offset = d for all. So \"bottom\" should be offsets = [d] = the \"left\"/\"top\" branch.\n\nSo indeed, in the current code, the \"left\"/\"top\" branch gives bottom alignment, and \"right\"/\"bottom\" branch gives top alignment.
